# Bartenura on Mishnah Peah

ברטנורא על משנה פאה

Link: [https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah)

חיבור התשתית הקדום של חז״ל, המתעד מגוון דעות הלכתיות ממסורת התורה שבעל פה. נחתם בשנת 200 לספירה לערך.

16th-century commentary printed in most editions of the Mishnah, including summaries of talmudic discussions and legal conclusions.

Sections: Chapter-Mishnah-Comment

Location: Mishnah/Rishonim on Mishnah/Bartenura/Seder Zeraim

Originally from [Sefaria](https://www.sefaria.org) via [TorahLM.org](https://torahlm.org) — see [licensing](https://torahlm.org/sefaria_license).

Version (He): Torat-Emet (Segments: 279, License: CC-BY-NC)

Version (En): Bartenura on Mishnah, trans. by Rabbi Robert Alpert, 2020 (Segments: 279)


## Chapter 1


### Mishnah 1

###### Bartenura on Mishnah Peah 1:1:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/1:1:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/1:1:1)


**אֵלּוּ דְבָרִים שֶׁאֵין לָהֶם שִׁעוּר.** מִדְּאוֹרַיְתָא, אֲבָל מִדְּרַבָּנָן יֵשׁ לָהֶם שִׁעוּר, דְּהָא תְּנַן בְּסֵיפָא אֵין פּוֹחֲתִין לַפֵּאָה מִשִּׁשִּׁים:


אלו דברים שאין להם שיעור – From the Torah [itself, it (the corner of the field) has no fixed measure], but from [the perspective of] the Rabbis, it has (another) measure, for it is taught at the end of the Mishnah (actually in Mishnah 2), “we don’t [bring any] less than one-sixtieth.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Peah 1:1:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/1:1:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/1:1:2)


**הַפֵּאָה.** שֶׁאָדָם חַיָּב לְהַנִּיחַ בְּסוֹף שָׂדֵהוּ לַעֲנִיִּים, כְּדִכְתִיב (ויקרא כג) לֹא תְכַלֶּה פְּאַת שָׂדְךָ בְּקֻצְרֶךָ:


הפאה – that an individual is obligated to leave at the edge of his field for the poor, as it is written (Leviticus 23:22), “[And when you reap the harvest of your land,] you shall not reap all the way to the edges of your field,[or gather the gleanings of your harvest; you shall leave them for the poor and the stranger: I the LORD am your God.].”


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Peah 1:1:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/1:1:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/1:1:3)


**וְהַבִּכּוּרִים.** דִּכְתִיב (שמות כג) רֵאשִׁית בִּכּוּרֵי אַדְמָתְךָ תָּבִיא בֵּית ה'‏ אֱלֹהֶיךָ, וְלֹא נָתְנָה תּוֹרָה בָּהֶם שִׁעוּר:


והביכורים – As it is written (Exodus 23:19), “The choice first fruits of your soil you shall bring to the house of the LORD your God…”(see also the exact same formulation in Exodus 34:26, which is the chapter source for the next Biblical quote), and the Torah did not provide for them a [fixed] measure.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Peah 1:1:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/1:1:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/1:1:4)


**וְהָרֵאָיוֹן.** שֶׁחַיָּב אָדָם לְהֵרָאוֹת בָּעֲזָרָה בְּשָׁלֹשׁ רְגָלִים, דִּכְתִיב (שם) שָׁלֹשׁ פְּעָמִים בַּשָּׁנָה יֵרָאֶה כָל זְכוּרְךָ, וְזֹאת הָרְאִיָּה אֵין לָהּ שִׁעוּר, דְּכָל אֵימַת דְּבָעֵי עַיֵּל וּמִתְחָזֵי וְנָפֵיק. פֵּרוּשׁ אַחֵר עוֹלַת רְאִיָּה וְשַׁלְמֵי חֲגִיגָה שֶׁחַיָּב לְהָבִיא כְּדִכְתִיב (שם) וְלֹא יֵרָאוּ פָנַי רֵיקָם, אֵין לָהּ שִׁעוּר מִדְּאוֹרַיְתָא דִּכְתִיב (דברים טז) אִישׁ כְּמַתְּנַת יָדוֹ, אֶלָּא שֶׁחֲכָמִים נָתְנוּ בָהּ שִׁעוּר, הָעוֹלָה מָעָה כֶּסֶף וַחֲגִיגָה שְׁתֵּי כֶסֶף:


והראיון – That a person is obligated to appear in the Temple court on the three Pilgrimage festivals (Passover, Shavuot and Sukkot), as it is written (Exodus 34:23), “Three times a year all your males shall appear [before the Sovereign LORD, the God of Israel,”(see also, Exodus 23:20 and Deuteronomy 16:16) for virtually identical language)” and this appearance in the Temple has no fixed measure, for whenever that he (i.e., that person) wishes to, he goes up and appears and then leaves. Another interpretation: The burnt offerings of “appearing” and the peace offerings [brought] as the festive offering of the visitors of the Temple on the festivals which [a person] is obligated to bring as it is written (Exodus 34:20) “…None shall appear before Me empty-handed” (see also Deuteronomy 16:16 for an identical command)., have no [fixed] measure from the Torah, as it is written (Deuteronomy 16:17), “but each with his own gift, [according to the blessing that the LORD your God has bestowed upon you],” but the Sages gave them a measure (see Mishnah Hagigah 1:2 in a dispute between the School of Shammai and the School of Hillel), the burnt offering is one m’ah silver and the festival offering is two silver pieces.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Peah 1:1:5

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/1:1:5](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/1:1:5)


**וּגְמִילוּת חֲסָדִים.** דִּבְגוּפוֹ, כְּגוֹן בִּקּוּר חוֹלִים וְלִקְבֹּר מֵתִים וְכַיּוֹצֵא בָהֶן, אֲבָל גְּמִילוּת חֲסָדִים דִּבְמָמוֹנוֹ, כְּגוֹן פִּדְיוֹן שְׁבוּיִם וּלְהַלְבִּישׁ עֲרוּמִים וּלְהַאֲכִיל אֶת הָרְעֵבִים וְכַיּוֹצֵא בָהֶן יֵשׁ לָהֶן שִׁעוּר, שֶׁיִּתֵּן בְּכָל פַּעַם שֶׁתָּבֹא מִצְוָה כָּזוֹ לְיָדוֹ חֲמִשִּׁית מִן הָרֶוַח שֶׁבִּנְכָסָיו, וְתוּ לֹא מִחַיֵּב, דְּהָכִי אָמְרִינַן הַמְבַזְבֵּז אַל יְבַזְבֵּז יוֹתֵר מֵחֹמֶשׁ. הִלְכָּךְ מִבָּעֵי לֵיהּ לְאִינָשׁ לְאַפְרוּשֵׁי חֲמִשִּׁית הָרֶוַח בְּכָל עֵת כְּדֵי שֶׁיְּהֵא מָצוּי כָּל זְמַן שֶׁיָּבֹא גְּמִילוּת חֶסֶד לְיָדוֹ שֶׁיְּקַיְּמֶנּוּ, וּבְהָכִי נָפֵיק יְדֵי חוֹבָתוֹ:


וגמילות חסדים – [and deeds of lovingkindness] of one’s body, such as visiting the sick and burying the dead and others like them, but deeds of lovingkindness [performed] with one’s money , such as the redemption of captives, the clothing of the naked and the feeding of the hungry and others like them, there is a [fixed] measure that one should give every time such a Mitzvah should come to his hand [to perform which is], one fifth of the profit of one’s possessions, and beyond that, one is not obligated [to give], as we say, a person who wants to be liberal (in his giving of tzedakah on a large scale), should not give more than twenty percent (see Ketubot 50a). Therefore, it is necessary for a person to separate out one fifth of his profit at all times so that it would be [readily] found whenever [an opportunity] to perform an act of lovingkindness comes to his hand, in order that he can sustain him (i.e. the less fortunate), and in that way, he fulfills his obligation.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Peah 1:1:6

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/1:1:6](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/1:1:6)


**וְתַלְמוּד תּוֹרָה.** אֵין לָהּ שִׁעוּר דִּכְתִיב (יהושע א) וְהָגִיתָ בּוֹ יוֹמָם וָלַיְלָה:


ותלמוד תורה – [The Study of Torah] has no fixed measure, as it is written (Joshua 1:8), “[Let not this Book of the Teaching cease from your lips,] but recite it day and night, [so that you may faithfully observe all that is written in it…].”


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Peah 1:1:7

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/1:1:7](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/1:1:7)


**וְתַלְמוּד תּוֹרָה כְּנֶגֶד כֻּלָּם.** שָׁקוּל כְּנֶגֶד כֻּלָּם:


ותלמוד תורה כנגד כולם – it (Torah study) is of equal importance to them all.



### Mishnah 2

###### Bartenura on Mishnah Peah 1:2:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/1:2:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/1:2:1)


**אֵין פּוֹחֲתִין לַפֵּאָה מִשִּׁשִּׁים.** מִדְּרַבָּנָן:


אין פוחתין לפאה מששים – from according to the Rabbis [no less than 1/60th]


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Peah 1:2:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/1:2:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/1:2:2)


**אַף עַל פִּי שֶׁאָמְרוּ אֵין לַפֵּאָה שִׁעוּר.** מִדְּאוֹרַיְתָא כִּדְאֲמָרָן:


אף על פי שאמרו אין לפאה שיעור – As we have stated (that there is no measure) from the Torah itself.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Peah 1:2:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/1:2:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/1:2:3)


**הַכֹּל לְפִי גֹדֶל הַשָּׂדֶה.** אִם שָׂדֵהוּ גְדוֹלָה וַעֲנִיִּים מֻעָטִים נוֹתֵן וּמוֹסִיף עַל שִׁשִּׁים לְפִי גֹדֶל שָׂדֵהוּ, וְאִם שָׂדֵהוּ קְטַנָּה וַעֲנִיִּים מְרֻבִּים מוֹסִיף עַל שִׁשִּׁים לְפִי רֹב הָעֲנִיִּים:


הכל לפי גודל השדה – If his field was large and the [number of] poor people few, he provides and increases upon the 1/60th measure – according to the size of his field; and if his field is small and the poor are many, he adds upon the 1/60th according to the multitude of the poor [in the midst of the community].


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Peah 1:2:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/1:2:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/1:2:4)


**וּלְפִי הָעֲנָוָה.** אִית דְּגָרְסֵי עֲנָוָה בְּוָי"ו, כְּלוֹמַר לְפִי רֹב עַנְוָתוֹ יַרְבֶּה לָעֲנִיִּים, לְשׁוֹן וְעַנְוָתְךָ תַרְבֵּנִי (תהלים יח). וְרַמְבַּ"ם פֵּרֵשׁ לְשׁוֹן עֲנִיָּה, כְּלוֹמַר מַה שֶּׁתַּעֲנֶה הָאָרֶץ. וְאִית דְּגָרְסֵי עֲנָבָה בְּבֵי"ת, וּפֵרְשׁוּ גֹדֶל הַגַּרְגְּרִים וְקָטְנָן, שֶׁאִם הָיוּ הַשִּׁבֳּלִים שֶׁל שָׂדֶה בְּמָקוֹם אֶחָד מְלֵאוֹת וְטוֹבוֹת וּבְמָקוֹם אַחֵר צְנוּמוֹת דַּקּוֹת לֹא יַנִּיחַ הַפֵּאָה כֻּלָּהּ מִן הָרָעוֹת אֶלָּא יְשַׁעֵר שִׁשִּׁים לָרַע וְלַיָּפֶה:


ולפי הענוה – [according to their humility]. There are those who have as their reading of the text ענוה – with a [letter] VAV, that is to say, according to his great measure of humility, he will increase [his gift] to the poor, based upon the language [found in] (Psalms 18:36), “Your care has made me great.” And Maimonides explained the expression as עניה [with a YOD], that is to say, how the earth will “respond” [as in Hosea 2:24 – “And the earth will respond with new grain and wine and oil…”]. And there are those who have as their reading of the text ענבה – with a BET, and its explanation is the large size of the grain and their smallness, for if the ears of corn of the field are in one place are full and good and in another shriveled [and] thin, he should not leave the corner of the field complete from the bad [grain], but he should estimate one-sixtieth for the bad [parts] and for the pleasing [parts].



### Mishnah 3

###### Bartenura on Mishnah Peah 1:3:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/1:3:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/1:3:1)


**מִתְּחִלַּת הַשָּׂדֶה וּמֵאֶמְצָעָהּ.** וְאֵין צָרִיךְ לְהַנִּיחַ פֵּאָה בְּסוֹף הַשָּׂדֶה, דִּכְתִיב (ויקרא יט) לֹא תְכַלֶּה פְּאַת שָׂדְךָ לִקְצֹר, אָמְרָה תּוֹרָה תֵּן פֵּאָה וְאַף עַל פִּי שֶׁיֵּשׁ לְךָ עֲדַיִן קָמָה לִקְצֹר:


מתחלת השדה ומאמצעה – And one does not need to place down the “corner” at the end of the field, as it is written (Leviticus 19:9): “[When you reap the harvest of your land,] you shall not reap all the way to the edges of your field…” said the Torah: Give the “corner” and even though you still have standing grain to reap.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Peah 1:3:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/1:3:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/1:3:2)


**וּבִלְבַד שֶׁיִּתֵּן בַּסּוֹף כַּשִּׁעוּר.** אַף עַל גַּב דְּרָבִי קְרָא בַּתְחִלָּה וּבָאֶמְצַע שֶׁיֵּשׁ תּוֹרַת פֵּאָה לְמַה שֶּׁנָּתַן, מִכָּל מָקוֹם לֹא מִפְטַר אֶלָּא אִם כֵּן נוֹתֵן בְּסוֹף הַשָּׂדֶה תַּשְׁלוּם שִׁעוּר אֶחָד מִשִּׁשִּׁים עִם מַה שֶּׁנָּתַן בָּאֶמְצַע וּבַתְּחִלָּה. כֵּן מוּכָח בַּיְרוּשַׁלְמִי. וְרַמְבַּ"ם פֵּרֵשׁ וּבִלְבַד שֶׁיִּתֵּן בַּסּוֹף כְּשִׁעוּר פֵּאָה הַצְּרִיכָה לְכָל הַשָּׂדֶה, שֶׁצָּרִיךְ שֶׁיַּנִּיחַ בְּסוֹף הַשָּׂדֶה אֶחָד מִשִּׁשִּׁים חוּץ מִמַּה שֶּׁהִנִּיחַ בַּתְּחִלָּה וּבָאֶמְצַע:


ובלבד שיתן בסוף כשיעור – Even though the Bible extends [the application (of the word פאה ) to include] the beginning and in the middle of the field which have [upon them] the Torah-obligation of [giving] Peah [upon it] to what he had given, nevertheless, he is only exempt if he gives at the end of the field a payment [equivalent to] the measurement of one-sixtieth, together with what he had given in the middle and at the beginning, such is derived in the Jerusalem Talmud. And Maimonides explained that only as long as he gives at the end according to the measure of PEAH that is required for the entire field, that he must set aside at the end of the field one-sixtieth apart from what he had left at the beginning and middle [of the field].


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Peah 1:3:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/1:3:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/1:3:3)


**אִם שִׁיֵּר קֶלַח אֶחָד.** בְּסוֹף הַשָּׂדֶה וְהִנִּיחַ אוֹתוֹ לְפֵאָה סוֹמֵךְ עָלָיו, וְכָל מַה שֶּׁהִנִּיחַ בָּאֶמְצַע וּבַתְּחִלָּה תּוֹרַת פֵּאָה עָלֶיהָ:


ואם שייר קלח אחד – At the edge of the field and left it for the [obligatory] “corner”, he relies upon it, and everything that he left in the middle and at the beginning, the law of “Peah” is upon it.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Peah 1:3:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/1:3:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/1:3:4)


**וְאִם לָאו.** שֶׁלֹּא הִנִּיחַ אֲפִלּוּ קֶלַח אֶחָד בְּסוֹף הַשָּׂדֶה לְפֵאָה, הֲרֵי מַה שֶּׁהִנִּיחַ בָּאֶמְצַע וּבַתְּחִלָּה אֵינָהּ חֲשׁוּבָה פֵּאָה אֶלָּא דִּין הֶפְקֵר יֵשׁ לָהּ. וּפָלֵיג אַדְּרַבִּי שִׁמְעוֹן דְּאָמַר דְּמִכָּל מָקוֹם הִיא חֲשׁוּבָה פֵּאָה אֶלָּא דְּלֹא מִפְטַר בַּעַל הַשָּׂדֶה בְּהָכִי. וַהֲלָכָה כְּרַבִּי שִׁמְעוֹן:


ואם לאו – If he did not leave even one stalk at the edge of the field for [the fulfillment of the commandment of] “the corner,” what he had left in the middle and at the beginning [of the field] are not considered “the corner” but it has only the principle of HEFKER/renunciation of ownership and he (Rabbi Yehuda) disagrees with [the opinion of] Rabbi Shimon who says, that nevertheless, it is considered “PEAH/the corner” - but that the owner of the field is not exempted [from his obligation to give PEAH] with this [alone]. And the Halakha is according to Rabbi Shimon.



### Mishnah 4

###### Bartenura on Mishnah Peah 1:4:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/1:4:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/1:4:1)


**כָּל דָּבָר שֶׁהוּא אֹכֶל.** לְמִעוּטֵי סְפִיחֵי סִטִּים, שֶׁאַף עַל פִּי שֶׁהֵן אֹכֶל עַל יְדֵי הַדְּחַק לָאו אֹכֶל מִקְרוּ וְאֵין חַיָּבִין בַּפֵּאָה, דִּכְתִיב (ויקרא יט) וּבְקֻצְרְכֶם, וְאֵין קְצִירָה אֶלָּא בְּדָבָר הָרָאוּי לַאֲכִילָה:


כל דבר שהוא אוכל – [Food] that excludes the after-growth of a plant producing a deep blue dye/woad, for even though they are “food” from an emergency perspective, it is not called “food” and one is not liable for [the mitzvah of] Peah/the corner of the field [regarding this product]., as it is written (Leviticus 19:9), “When you reap the harvest [of your land, you shall not reap all the way to the edges of your field, or gather the gleanings of your harvest],” there is no “harvest” other than that which is appropriate for eating.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Peah 1:4:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/1:4:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/1:4:2)


**וְנִשְׁמָר.** לְמִעוּטֵי הֶפְקֵר שֶׁאֵין לוֹ שׁוֹמְרִים שֶׁאֵינוֹ חַיָּב בַּפֵּאָה, דִּכְתִיב (שם) לֶעָנִי וְלַגֵּר תַּעֲזֹב אוֹתָם, פְּרָט לְהֶפְקֵר שֶׁכְּבָר נֶעֱזַב:


ונשמר – Excluding that which is ownerless/HEFKER which has no guardians, is not liable for [the Mitzvah of] the “corner of the field/PEAH,” as it is written (Leviticus 19:10), “…you shall leave them for the poor and stranger [I the LORD am your God],” excluding that which is ownerless, which has already forsaken.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Peah 1:4:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/1:4:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/1:4:3)


**וְגִדּוּלָיו מִן הָאָרֶץ.** לְמִעוּטֵי כְּמֵהִין וּפִטְרִיּוֹת, שֶׁאֵין לָהֶם שֹׁרֶשׁ בָּאָרֶץ וְגִדּוּלֵיהֶם מִן הָאֲוִיר, שֶׁאֵין חַיָּבִין בַּפֵּאָה דִּכְתִיב (שם) קְצִיר אַרְצְכֶם:


וגידולו מן הארץ –[something that grows in the ground], excluding morils and truffles lack roots in the land, and their growth is from the air which are not liable for [the Mitzvah of] the corner of the field/PEAH, as it is written (Leviticus 19:9), “[When you reap] the harvest of your land…”


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Peah 1:4:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/1:4:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/1:4:4)


**וּלְקִיטָתוֹ כְּאֶחָד.** לְמִעוּטֵי תְּאֵנָה וְכַיּוֹצֵא בָהּ מִן הָאִילָנוֹת שֶׁנִּלְקָטִים הַפֵּרוֹת שֶׁמִּתְבַּשְּׁלִים בָּהֶם רִאשׁוֹן רִאשׁוֹן, דִּכְתִיב (שם) קְצִיר, מַשְׁמַע דָּבָר הַנִּקְצָר הַרְבֵּה בְּיַחַד:


ולקיטתו כאחד – [and such fruits harvested all at once (not singly as they become ripe)] excluding figs and those [fruits] similar to them from the trees when the fruits are harvested first as they ripen , as it is written (Leviticus 19:9), “[When you reap] the harvest [of your land]…,” implying when many of something are harvested together.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Peah 1:4:5

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/1:4:5](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/1:4:5)


**וּמַכְנִיסוֹ לְקִיּוּם.** לְמִעוּטֵי יָרָק שֶׁאֵינוֹ מִתְקַיֵּם אֶלָּא מִתְעַפֵּשׁ מִיָּד, דִּכְתִיב (שם כג) פְּאַת שָׂדְךָ בְּקֻצְרֶךָ, וְאֵין הַיָּרָק נִקְצָר:


ומכניסו לקיום – excluding vegetables which do not endure as they grow moldy immediately, as it is written (Leviticus 23:22), “[And when you reap the harvest of your land], you shall not reap all the way to the edges your field, [or gather the gleanings of your harvest]…” as vegetation is not reaped.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Peah 1:4:6

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/1:4:6](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/1:4:6)


**וְהַתְּבוּאָה וְהַקִּטְנִיּוֹת.** תְּבוּאָה מֵחֲמֵשֶׁת הַמִּינִים, חִטָּה וּשְׂעוֹרָה וְכוּסֶמֶת שִׁבֹּלֶת שׁוּעָל וְשִׁיפוֹן קִטְנִיּוֹת פּוֹלִין וַאֲפוּנִים וַעֲדָשִׁים וְדוֹמֵיהֶן:


תבואה והקטניות – grain of the five kinds – wheat, barley, spelt, oats and rye. KITNIYOT/pulse beans, beans and lentils and things similar to this.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Peah 1:4:7

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/1:4:7](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/1:4:7)


**בַּכְּלָל הַזֶּה.** לְהִתְחַיֵּב בַּפֵּאָה כְּשֶׁיִּשְׁלְמוּ בָהֶם כָּל הַתְּנָאִים הַלָּלוּ:


בכלל הזה – To be obligated in [the Mitzvah of] PEAH/the corner of the field, when they will complete all of these conditions.



### Mishnah 5

###### Bartenura on Mishnah Peah 1:5:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/1:5:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/1:5:1)


**הָאוֹג.** אִילָן שֶׁהַפְּרִי שֶׁלּוֹ אָדוֹם, וְקוֹרִין לוֹ בְּלַעַ"ז קורנ"י אולי"ס. וְרַמְבַּ"ם פֵּרֵשׁ שֶׁהוּא צֶמַח שֶׁמְּעַבְּדִים בּוֹ הָעוֹרוֹת וְקוֹרִין לוֹ בַּעֲרָבִי סומא"ק, וְהָאֶשְׁכֹּלוֹת שֶׁלּוֹ רְאוּיִין לַאֲכִילָה. וְלָאו דַּוְקָא אֵלּוּ שֶׁמָּנָה הַתַּנָּא חַיָּבִים בַּפֵּאָה וְלֹא מִינֵי אִילָנוֹת אֲחֵרִים, אֶלָּא אֵלּוּ וְדוֹמֵיהֶן קָאָמַר:


האוג – [the red berry of the Venus’ summachtree] – a tree whose fruit is red which is called in a foreign language KURNI ULIM. And Maimonides explained that it is a plant that dresses hides and is called in Arabic SIMMAC, and its clusters are appropriate for eating, but not specifically these which the Tanna [of our Mishnah] enumerated are liable for [the Mitzvah of] PEAH/the corner of the field and not other kinds of trees other than these, rather these and anything similar to them are mentioned.



### Mishnah 6

###### Bartenura on Mishnah Peah 1:6:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/1:6:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/1:6:1)


**לְעוֹלָם הוּא נוֹתֵן מִשּׁוּם פֵּאָה.** מִי שֶׁלֹּא הִנִּיחַ פֵּאָה בַּשָּׂדֶה בַּמְחֻבָּר חַיָּב לְהַפְרִישׁ מִן הַתָּלוּשׁ, וְהַפֵּאָה שֶׁמַּפְרִישׁ מִן הַתָּלוּשׁ וְנוֹתֵן לָעֲנִיִּים פְּטוּרָה מִן הַמַּעַשְׂרוֹת:


לעולם הוא נותן משום פאה – Whomever did not leave the corner of the field with attached produce, is liable to set aside [for the commandment of Peah] from what is detached from the soil. And that corner of the field’s produce that one sets aside from what is detached from the soil is given to the poor and is exempt from tithing.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Peah 1:6:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/1:6:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/1:6:2)


**עַד שֶׁיְּמָרֵחַ.** שֶׁיֵּעָשֶׂה כְּרִי וְצִבּוּר שֶׁל תְּבוּאָה, אֲבָל אִם בָּא לְהַפְרִישׁ פֵּאָה אַחַר הַמֵּרוּחַ צָרִיךְ שֶׁיַּפְרִישׁ הַתְּרוּמָה וְהַמַּעַשְׂרוֹת תְּחִלָּה וְאַחַר כָּךְ יִטֹּל הַפֵּאָה, שֶׁאֵין פָּטוּר מִן הַמַּעַשְׂרוֹת אֶלָּא הַפֵּאָה שֶׁנִּטְּלָה קֹדֶם הַמֵּרוּחַ:


עד שימרח – He should make a heap and pile of grain. But if one came to separate out the corner [of the field] after giving the pile an even shape, he must first separate out the heave-offering/sacred donation to the Kohen and the tithes and afterwards take the corner, for nothing is exempt from tithes other than the corner that was taken prior to giving the pile an even shape.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Peah 1:6:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/1:6:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/1:6:3)


**וְנוֹתֵן מִשּׁוּם הֶפְקֵר וּפָטוּר מִן הַמַּעַשְׂרוֹת.** (משום הפקר) הַמַּפְקִיר תְּבוּאָתוֹ וּבָא אַחֵר וְזָכָה בָהּ, אֵין הַזּוֹכֶה בָהּ חַיָּב בְּמַעַשְׂרוֹת דִּכְתִיב (דברים יד) וּבָא הַלֵּוִי כִּי אֵין לוֹ חֵלֶק וְנַחֲלָה עִמָּךְ, יָצָא הֶפְקֵר שֶׁיָּדְךָ וְיָדוֹ שָׁוִין, אֲבָל הַמַּפְקִיר תְּבוּאָתוֹ לְאַחַר הַמֵּרוּחַ אֵין הַהֶפְקֵר הַהוּא פּוֹטֵר מִן הַמַּעֲשֵׂר:


ונותן משום הפקר ופטור מן המעשרות – He who makes his grain ownerless and another [person] comes and takes possession of it, the person who benefits from it is not liable for tithes, as it is written (Deuteronomy 14:29): “Then the Levite, who has no hereditary portion as you have…” excluding the situation where it is ownerless, where “your hand” and “his hand” are equivalent. But an individual who declares his grain ownerless after giving the pile an even shape, this [grain being] ownerless does not exempt him from tithing.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Peah 1:6:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/1:6:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/1:6:4)


**וּמַאֲכִיל לַבְּהֵמָה וְכוּ'.** וַאֲפִלּוּ הוּא עַצְמוֹ מֻתָּר לֶאֱכֹל אֲכִילַת עֲרַאי קֹדֶם מֵרוּחַ, אֲבָל הַבְּהֵמָה אוֹכֶלֶת אֲפִלּוּ אֲכִילַת קֶבַע:


ומאכיל לבהמה – And even if he himself is permitted to eat an incidental meal (i.e., snack) prior to giving the pile an even shape, but the cattle eat even a regular meal.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Peah 1:6:5

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/1:6:5](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/1:6:5)


**נוֹטֵל מִן הַגֹּרֶן וְזוֹרֵעַ וְכוּ'.** דְּמִן הַתּוֹרָה הַזּוֹרֵעַ פָּטוּר מִן הַמַּעֲשֵׂר אֲפִלּוּ לְאַחַר מֵרוּחַ דִּכְתִיב (שם) עַשֵּׂר תְּעַשֵּׂר וְאָכַלְתָּ, וְזוֹרֵעַ לֹא קָרִינַן בֵּיהּ וְאָכַלְתָּ, מִיהוּ מִדְּרַבָּנָן חַיָּב. סְבִירָא לֵיהּ לְרַבִּי עֲקִיבָא דְּקֹדֶם מֵרוּחַ פָּטוּר מִן הַמַּעֲשֵׂר אֲפִלּוּ מִדְּרַבָּנָן. וְאֵין הֲלָכָה כְּרַבִּי עֲקִיבָא:


נוטל מן הגורן וזורע וכו' – since from the Torah, one who sows the field is exempt from tithing even after giving the pile an even shape, as it is written (Deuteronomy 14:22-23): “You shall set aside every year a tenth part [of all the yield of your sowing that is brought from the field]. You shall consume [the tithes of your new grain and wine and oil…].” But we do not call one who sows “you shall consume,” but according to the Rabbis, he is liable. Rabbi Akiba thinks that prior to giving the pile an even shape, one is exempt from tithes, even according to the Rabbis. But the Halakha is not according to Rabbi Akiba.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Peah 1:6:6

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/1:6:6](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/1:6:6)


**שֶׁלָּקְחוּ אֶת הַגֹּרֶן.** שֶׁקָּנוּ תְּבוּאָה מִן הַגֹּרֶן, וְקָנְסוּ חֲכָמִים שֶׁיִּהְיוּ חַיָּבִים לְהַפְרִישׁ תְּרוּמוֹת וּמַעַשְׂרוֹת וְלָתֵת אוֹתָם לְכֹהֲנִים וּלְוִיִּם אֲחֵרִים כְּדֵי שֶׁלֹּא יִהְיוּ קוֹפְצִים לִקְנוֹת תְּבוּאָה אוֹ יַיִן לַגִּתּוֹת וְלַגְּרָנוֹת. וְאִם קָנוּ קֹדֶם מֵרוּחַ, שֶׁעֲדַיִן לֹא הֻקְבְּעָה תְּבוּאָה זוֹ לְמַעֲשֵׂר, הַמַּעַשְׂרוֹת שֶׁלָּהֶם:


שלקחו את הגורן – who purchased grain from the threshing floor/granary, and the Sages fined him so that they would be liable to separate out the heave-offerings and tithes and give them to other Kohanim and Levites in order that they would not jump to purchase grain or wine in the wine-presses or granaries. But if they purchased it prior to giving the pile an even shape, this grain was not yet appointed [as ready] to be given as tithes, and the tithes are theirs.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Peah 1:6:7

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/1:6:7](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/1:6:7)


**הַמַּקְדִּישׁ וּפוֹדֶה.** הַמַּקְדִּישׁ גָּרְנוֹ וּפְדָאוֹ מִיַּד הַגִּזְבָּר, וְהוּא גָּדִישׁ אוֹ קָמָה שֶׁלֹּא הֻקְבַּע לְמַעֲשֵׂר כְּשֶׁהָיָה בְּיַד הַהֶקְדֵּשׁ, חַיָּב הַפּוֹדֶה לְהוֹצִיא מִמֶּנּוּ הַמַּעַשְׂרוֹת. אֲבָל אִם נִתְמַרֵחַ הַגֹּרֶן בְּיַד הַגִּזְבָּר הוֹאִיל וּבְשָׁעָה שֶׁהָיָה רָאוּי שֶׁיֻּקְבַּע לְמַעֲשֵׂר דְּהַיְנוּ בִּזְמַן הַמֵּרוּחַ הָיְתָה בְּיַד הֶקְדֵּשׁ, אֵין הַפּוֹדֶה חַיָּב לְהוֹצִיא מִמֶּנּוּ מַעַשְׂרוֹת, שֶׁהַהֶקְדֵּשׁ פָּטוּר מִן הַמַּעַשְׂרוֹת:


המקדיש ופודה – A person who sanctifies his granary [to the Temple] and redeems it from the hand of the treasurer, and it is a stack of grain or standing corn that was not yet appointed [as ready] to be given as tithes when it was in the hands of the sacred Temple property, the one who redeems it must take out from it the tithes. But if the granary had been shaped into an even pile by the hand of the treasurer, since at the time that it was worthy of being appointed [as ready] to be given as tithes, that is, at the time of when it had been shaped into an even pile, it was in the hand of the Temple, the redeemer is not liable to remove from it the tithes as that which is dedicated to the Temple is exempt from tithing.



## Chapter 2


### Mishnah 1

###### Bartenura on Mishnah Peah 2:1:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/2:1:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/2:1:1)


**אֵלּוּ מַפְסִיקִין.** בֵּין שָׂדֶה לְשָׂדֶה וְיִהְיוּ נֶחֱשָׁבִים כִּשְׁתֵּי שָׂדוֹת, לְעִנְיַן שֶׁאִם הִנִּיחַ פֵּאָה בְּאַחַת מֵהֶן עַל חֲבֶרְתָּהּ לֹא עָלְתָה לוֹ לְפֵאָה, דִּכְתִיב (ויקרא יט) פְּאַת שָׂדְךָ, שֶׁלֹּא יַנִּיחַ פֵּאָה מִשָּׂדֶה לַחֲבֶרְתָּהּ:


אלו מפסיקין – between one field and another and they would be considered like two fields, regarding [the case] that if he left the corner fallow in one of them over its neighbor, he did not fulfill [the Mitzvah] of Peah, as it is written (Leviticus 19:9): “….you shall not reap all the way to the edges of your field…,” for he did not leave the corner fallow from one field to its neighbor.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Peah 2:1:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/2:1:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/2:1:2)


**הַנַּחַל.** נָהָר:


הנחל – river


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Peah 2:1:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/2:1:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/2:1:3)


**וְהַשְּׁלוּלִית.** אַמַּת הַמַּיִם שֶׁמְּחַלֶּקֶת שָׁלָל לַאֲגַפֶּיהָ שֶׁאַמּוֹת אֲחֵרוֹת שֶׁל מַיִם שׁוֹתוֹת מִמֶּנָּה:


שלולית – canal that imparts booty (alluvium) to its banks (see Talmud Bava Kamma 61a), that other canals drink from it.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Peah 2:1:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/2:1:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/2:1:4)


**וְדֶרֶךְ הַיָּחִיד.** אַרְבַּע אַמּוֹת:


ודרך היחיד – four cubits


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Peah 2:1:5

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/2:1:5](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/2:1:5)


**וְדֶרֶךְ הָרַבִּים.** שֵׁשׁ עֶשְׂרֵה אַמָּה, וּמִשּׁוּם סֵיפָא נָקַט לַהּ דְּבָעֵי לְמִתְנֵי סֵיפָא שֶׁכָּל אֵלּוּ אֵין מַפְסִיקִין בְּאִילָן, וְקָא מַשְׁמַע לָן דַּאֲפִלּוּ דֶּרֶךְ הָרַבִּים שֶׁהִיא רְחָבָה שֵׁשׁ עֶשְׂרֵה אַמָּה אֵינָהּ מַפְסֶקֶת בְּאִילָן:


ודרך הרבים – sixteen cubits, and because of the concluding section [of the Mishnah] it used it, for it was necessary for the Mishnah to teach the ending section that all of these do not form a division at a tree. And it comes to tell us that even the communal path which is sixteen cubits wide does not form a division at a tree.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Peah 2:1:6

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/2:1:6](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/2:1:6)


**שְׁבִיל הָרַבִּים וּשְׁבִיל הַיָּחִיד.** שְׁבִיל זוּטָר טוּבָא, דְּשָׁקִיל כַּרְעָא וּמָנַח כַּרְעָא. וְאַשְׁמְעִינַן דַּאֲפִלּוּ שְׁבִיל דְּרַבִּים אִם הוּא קָבוּעַ לִימוֹת הַחַמָּה וְלִימוֹת הַגְּשָׁמִים, כְּלוֹמַר שֶׁמְּהַלְּכִין בּוֹ אֲפִלּוּ בִּזְמַן שֶׁהַשָּׂדוֹת זְרוּעוֹת בִּימוֹת הַגְּשָׁמִים, מַפְסִיק בֵּין הַשָּׂדוֹת הַזְּרוּעוֹת וְאִי לֹא, לֹא מַפְסִיק:


שביל הרבים ושביל היחיד – many small paths that took a foot and left a foot, and we learn that even the communal path if it is fixed for the days of sunshine (i.e., summer months) and the days of rain (i.e., winter months), that means to say, that people walk on it, even at the time when the fields are sown during the rainy seasons that separate between sown fields, and if not, they do not separate.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Peah 2:1:7

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/2:1:7](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/2:1:7)


**הַבּוּר.** שָׂדֶה שֶׁאֵינָהּ זְרוּעָה. וְהָאֲדָמָה לֹא תֵשָׁם (בראשית מז) מְתַרְגְּמִינַן וְאַרְעָא לֹא תְבוּר:


הבור – a field that is not sown [as it is written] (Genesis 47:19): “that the land may not become a waste,” which we translate [in Aramaic]: “and the land lay waste.”


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Peah 2:1:8

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/2:1:8](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/2:1:8)


**וְהַנִּיר.** חֲרִישָׁה. כְּמוֹ נִירוּ לָכֶם נִיר (ירמיה ד):


והניר – ploughing, like (Jeremiah 4:3): “Break up the untilled ground, [and do not sow among thorns].”


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Peah 2:1:9

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/2:1:9](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/2:1:9)


**וְזֶרַע אַחֵר.** כְּגוֹן שֶׁשְּׁתֵּי הַשָּׂדוֹת זְרוּעִים חִטִּים וּבֵינֵיהֶם קַרְקַע זָרוּעַ מִין אַחֵר. וְשִׁעוּר רֹחַב הַבּוּר וְהַנִּיר וְזֶרַע אַחֵר כְּדֵי שְׁלֹשָׁה תְּלָמִים שֶׁל מַעֲנִית הַמַּחֲרֵשָׁה:


וזרע אחר – like the example of two fields that are sown with wheat and between them is a piece of land sown with another kind. And the measurement of the width of the fallow land or the newly broken land and another seed is of three furrows with the handle of the plow.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Peah 2:1:10

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/2:1:10](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/2:1:10)


**וְהַקּוֹצֵר לְשַׁחַת מַפְסִיק.** דְּקָסָבַר כָּל לְשַׁחַת לָאו קְצִירָה הִיא, דְּלֹא חָשְׁבִינַן לַהּ אַתְחַלְתָּא דִקְצִירָה. שַׁחַת, תְּבוּאָה שֶׁלֹּא הֵבִיאָה שְׁלִישׁ, וְקוֹצְרִים אוֹתָהּ לְהַאֲכִיל לַבְּהֵמוֹת. וְאֵין הֲלָכָה כְּרַבִּי מֵאִיר:


והקוצר לשחת מפסיק – for he (i.e., Rabbi Meir) holds that a person who harvests young grain [for use as fodder] – is not for harvesting, and we don’t consider it as the beginning of harvesting. Corn that is used as fodder at its earliest stage which is grain that did not bring forth a third, and we harvest it to feed it to animals. But the Halakha is not according to Rabbi Meir.



### Mishnah 2

###### Bartenura on Mishnah Peah 2:2:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/2:2:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/2:2:1)


**אַמַּת הַמַּיִם שֶׁאֵינָהּ יְכוֹלָה לְהִקָּצֵר כְּאַחַת.** שֶׁהִיא רְחָבָה כָּל כָּךְ עַד שֶׁהָעוֹמֵד בָּאֶמְצַע אֵינוֹ יָכוֹל לִקְצֹר מִכָּאן וּמִכָּאן:


אמת המים שאינה יכולה להקצר כאחת – which was so wide that until one stands in the middle [of the channel] , he is unable to reap from one side or the other.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Peah 2:2:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/2:2:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/2:2:2)


**רַבִּי יְהוּדָה אוֹמֵר מַפְסֶקֶת.** וּפָלֵיג אַתַּנָּא קַמָּא דְּאָמַר לְעֵיל עַל שְׁלוּלִית דְּהַיְנוּ אַמַּת הַמַּיִם דִּבְכָל עִנְיָן מַפְסִיק, וְרַבִּי יְהוּדָה סָבַר דְּאִם יְכוֹלָה לְהִקָּצֵר כְּאַחַת אֵינָהּ מַפְסֶקֶת. וַהֲלָכָה כְּרַבִּי יְהוּדָה:


ר' יהודה אומר מפסקת – And he disagrees with the First Tanna, that he said above regarding the pool, that is the canal that in every manner it forms a division; but Rabbi Yehuda holds that if he is able to reap as one, it does not divide. And the Halakha is according to Rabbi Yehuda.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Peah 2:2:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/2:2:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/2:2:3)


**וְכֹל הֶהָרִים אֲשֶׁר בַּמַּעְדֵּר יֵעָדֵרוּן.** הַר שֶׁחוּדוֹ זָקוּף וְאֵין בָּקָר בְּכֵלָיו יְכוֹלִין לַעֲבֹר שָׁם, וּמַפְסִיק בֵּין שְׁתֵּי הַשָּׂדוֹת:


וכל ההרים אשר במעדר יעדרון – A mountain whose thin-point is upright and cattle with its utensils are unable to to pass there, and there is a division between the two fields.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Peah 2:2:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/2:2:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/2:2:4)


**הוּא נוֹתֵן פֵּאָה לַכֹּל.** כְּלוֹמַר נוֹתֵן פֵּאָה אַחַת לְכָל שְׁתֵּי הַשָּׂדוֹת וְלֹא חָשִׁיב הֶפְסֵק, הוֹאִיל וְהֵם נֶעְדָּרִים בַּמַּעְדֵּר, כְּלוֹמַר שֶׁבְּנֵי אָדָם חוֹפְרִים אוֹתוֹ הַר בִּכְלִי שֶׁחוֹפְרִים בּוֹ אֶת הַקַּרְקַע, אֵין זֶה הֶפְסֵק. שֶׁהָרוֹאֶה אוֹמֵר אֵין זֶה קַרְקַע בּוּר אֶלָּא לְמָחָר חוֹפְרִים אוֹתוֹ בַּמַּעְדֵּר וְזוֹרְעִים אוֹתוֹ וּשְׁתֵּי הַשָּׂדוֹת הֵם אַחַת:


הוא נותן פאה לכל – that is to say, he gives provides one “Peah”/corner for the two fields and it is not considered a division, for since they are hoed with a mattock, that is to say, that people dig the mountain with the utensil that they dig up the ground, this is not a division. For the person who sees it says that this not uncultivated ground, but the next day, they hoe it with a mattock and seed it and the two fields which are as one.



### Mishnah 3

###### Bartenura on Mishnah Peah 2:3:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/2:3:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/2:3:1)


**הַכֹּל מַפְסִיק לַזְּרָעִים.** מְפָרֵשׁ בַּגְּמָרָא בַּיְרוּשַׁלְמִי דְּהַכֹּל לַאֲתוֹיֵי סֶלַע שֶׁהָיָה עוֹבֵר עַל פְּנֵי כָּל הַשָּׂדֶה, אִם צָרִיךְ הוּא לַעֲקֹר אֶת הַמַּחֲרֵשָׁה מִצַּד זֶה כְּדֵי לָתֵת אוֹתָהּ לְצַד זֶה, מַפְסִיק:


הכל מפסיק לזרעים – It is explains in the Gemara of the Jerusalem Talmud (Peah 17a) that the word הכל/all of these – includes a rock hat would pass over the face of the entire field. If it is necessary to uproot the plough from this side in order to place it on that side, one makes a division.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Peah 2:3:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/2:3:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/2:3:2)


**אֶלָּא גָדֵר.** גָּבֹהַּ עֲשָׂרָה טְפָחִים:


אלא גדר – ten handbreadths high.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Peah 2:3:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/2:3:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/2:3:3)


**שֵׂעָר כּוֹתֵשׁ.** עַנְפֵי הָאִילָן קְרוּיִין שֵׂעָר. וּפֵרוּשׁ שֵׂעָר כּוֹתֵשׁ, שֶׁהַנּוֹפוֹת שֶׁל אִילָנוֹת מִתְעָרְבִין זֶה עִם זֶה לְמַעְלָה מִן הַגָּדֵר כֶּעֱלִי זֶה הַתָּקוּעַ בַּמַּכְתֵּשׁ:


שער כותש – the branches of a tree are called שער. And the explanation is a thick hair crown, i.e., ramifications forming a sort of arbor – that the boughs of the trees combine one with the other above from the fence like these leaves that are caught in the mortar-shaped cavity. [The Gemara asks: Does this mean, like the pestle in the mortar (i.e., the partition is formed by a depression in the ground between the two fields, out of which the fence rises), or does it mean, pressing up (overgrowing) the fence? The ‘hair (ramification) presses, and not the ‘fence presence’, it is evident that it means overgrowing the fence.’].



### Mishnah 4

###### Bartenura on Mishnah Peah 2:4:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/2:4:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/2:4:1)


**כָּל הָרוֹאִים זֶה אֶת זֶה.** כָּל זְמַן שֶׁהָעוֹמֵד אֵצֶל אֶחָד מֵהָאִילָנוֹת יָכוֹל לִרְאוֹת הָאִילָן הָאַחֵר, אַף עַל פִּי שֶׁיֵּשׁ בֵּינֵיהֶן גָּדֵר, אֵינוֹ מַפְסִיק, וְלוֹקֵחַ פֵּאָה מֵאִילָן אֶחָד עַל חֲבֵרוֹ:


כל הרואים זה את זה – All the while that he stands near one of the trees, he can see the other tree; even though there is a fence between them, it does not divide, and he takes [one portion of] Peah from the one tree on behalf of its neighbor.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Peah 2:4:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/2:4:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/2:4:2)


**לַזֵּיתִים שֶׁהָיוּ לָהֶם בְּכָל רוּחַ.** פֵּאָה אַחַת הָיוּ נוֹתְנִים עַל כָּל הַזֵּיתִים שֶׁהָיוּ לָהֶם לְצַד מִזְרָחָהּ שֶׁל עִיר, וּפֵאָה אַחֶרֶת עַל כָּל הַזֵּיתִים שֶׁהָיוּ לָהֶם לְצַד מַעֲרָבָהּ, וְכֵן לְאַרְבַּע רוּחוֹת:


לזיתים שהיו להם בכל רוח – They would give [one portion of] Peah on all the olives that they had on the eastern side of the city, and another [portion of] Peah for all the olives that they had on eh western side, and similarly for the four directions.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Peah 2:4:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/2:4:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/2:4:3)


**שֶׁהָיוּ לָהֶם בְּכָל הָעִיר.** אֵין נוֹתְנִים כִּי אִם פֵּאָה אַחַת לְכֻלָּן, אַף עַל פִּי שֶׁאֵין רוֹאִין זֶה אֶת זֶה, וְאֵין הֲלָכָה כְּרַבִּי אֱלִיעֶזֶר בְּרַבִּי צָדוֹק שֶׁאָמַר מִשְּׁמוֹ, אֲבָל הֲלָכָה כְּרַבָּן גַּמְלִיאֵל:


שהיו להם בכל העיר – they do not give other than one [portion of] Peah for all of them, even though they don’t see each other, but the Halakha is not according to Rabbi Eliezer the son of Rabbi Tzadok who quoted him. But the Halakha is according to Rabban Gamaliel.



### Mishnah 5

###### Bartenura on Mishnah Peah 2:5:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/2:5:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/2:5:1)


**שְׁנֵי מִינֵי חִטִּין.** כְּגוֹן שְׁחַמְתִּית וּלְבָנָה:


שני מיני חיטין – such as reddish, dark-colored wheat and white-colored wheat



### Mishnah 6

###### Bartenura on Mishnah Peah 2:6:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/2:6:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/2:6:1)


**הַלַּבְלָר.** הַסּוֹפֵר:


הלבלר – the scribe


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Peah 2:6:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/2:6:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/2:6:2)


**הַזּוּגוֹת.** הֵם כָּל אוֹתָן שְׁנַיִם שְׁנַיִם הַנִּזְכָּרִים בְּפֶרֶק קַמָּא דְמַסֶּכֶת אָבוֹת, שֶׁקִּבְּלוּ שְׁנַיִם מִפִּי שְׁנַיִם [עַד] מִפִּי שִׁמְעוֹן הַצַּדִּיק:


הזוגות – they are those mentioned two-by-two in the first chapter of Mishnah Avot (from Mishnah 4), where two [individuals] received [the tradition] from two [others] [until] from the mouth of Shimon HaTzaddik/the Righteous.



### Mishnah 7

###### Bartenura on Mishnah Peah 2:7:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/2:7:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/2:7:1)


**שָׂדֶה שֶׁקְּצָרוּהָ כּוּתִים.** לְעַצְמָן. וְלֹא שֶׁקְּצָרוּהָ פֹּעֲלֵי כּוּתִים לְיִשְׂרָאֵל, דְּאָז הֲוֵי כְּאִלּוּ קְצָרוּהָ יִשְׂרָאֵל:


שדה שקצרוה גוים – for themselves. But the heathen workers did not harvest it for an Israelite, for then, it is as if an Israelite harvested it.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Peah 2:7:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/2:7:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/2:7:2)


**קִרְסְמוּהָ נְמָלִים.** דֶּרֶךְ הַנְּמָלָה לַחְתֹּךְ קְנֵה הַשִּׁבֹּלֶת מִלְּמַטָּה. וְלָזֶה קוֹרִים קִרְסוּם, לְשׁוֹן יְכַרְסְמֶנָּה חֲזִיר מִיָּעַר (תהלים פ):


קרסמוה נמלים – it is the manner of ants to sever the branch of an ear of corn from the bottom, and this is called the plucking of tops off, in the language of (Psalms 80:14): “wild boars gnaw at it.”


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Peah 2:7:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/2:7:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/2:7:3)


**פְּטוּרָה.** דִּכְתִיב גַּבֵּי פֵּאָה וּבְקֻצְרְכֶם, עַד שֶׁתִּהְיוּ אַתֶּם הַקֹּצְרִים:


פטורה – as it is written concerning the corner (Leviticus 23:22): “and when you reap [the harvest of your land…” until you, yourselves will be the reapers/harvesters.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Peah 2:7:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/2:7:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/2:7:4)


**שֶׁחוֹבַת הַקָּצִיר בַּקָּמָה.** כְּלוֹמַר חוֹבַת הַפֵּאָה שֶׁל זֶה הִנִּיחָהּ בַּקָּמָה. וּנְהִי דְאִם כִּלָּה שָׂדֶה, חָזְרָה פֵּאָה לָעֳמָרִים וְחַיָּב לְהַפְרִישׁ פֵּאָה מִן הָעֳמָרִים, הַנֵּי מִלֵּי הֵיכָא דְכִלָּה הוּא, אֲבָל הָכָא הוּא לֹא כִלָּה:


שחובת הקציר בקמה – that is to say, the obligation for the corner of this that is left standing, and assuming that if the field was destroyed, the corner returned to the sheaves and he is liable to separate the corner from the sheaves; these words apply where the field was destroyed, but here it was not destroyed.



### Mishnah 8

###### Bartenura on Mishnah Peah 2:8:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/2:8:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/2:8:1)


**הַלּוֹקֵחַ נוֹתֵן פֵּאָה לַכֹּל.** שֶׁחוֹבַת הַפֵּאָה מִמַּה שֶּׁקָּצַר נִשְׁאַר בְּזֶה הַחֵצִי שֶׁקָּנָה הַלּוֹקֵחַ, וַהֲוֵי כְּאִלּוּ לֹא מָכַר לוֹ אֶלָּא מַה שֶּׁנִּשְׁאַר בַּשָּׂדֶה אַחַר שֶׁיּוֹצִיא מִמֶּנָּה הַפֵּאָה שֶׁרָאוּי לְהוֹצִיא מֵאוֹתָהּ שָׂדֶה. וְכֵן הַפּוֹדֶה מִיַּד הַגִּזְבָּר מוֹצִיא מִן הַחֵצִי שֶׁפָּדָה הַפֵּאָה הָרְאוּיָה לַשָּׂדֶה כֻלָּהּ:


הלוקח נותן פאה לכל – that the obligation of the corner from what he had harvested remains in that one-half that the purchaser bought, and it is as if he did not sell him other than what had remained in the field after he removed from it the appropriate corner to be removed from that field, and similarly, one who redeems from the hand of the treasurer removes from the one-half that he redeemed the appropriate corner for the entire field.



## Chapter 3


### Mishnah 1

###### Bartenura on Mishnah Peah 3:1:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/3:1:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/3:1:1)


**מַלְבְּנוֹת.** עֲרוּגוֹת מְרֻבָּעוֹת. וּבִשְׁבִיל שֶׁעֲשׂוּיוֹת כִּדְפוּסֵי הַלְּבֵנוֹת קְרוּיִין מַלְבְּנוֹת:


מלבנות – a square garden-bed, and because they were made like the white frames, they are called garden-beds/plots.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Peah 3:1:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/3:1:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/3:1:2)


**שֶׁבֵּין הַזֵּיתִים.** וְהוּא הַדִּין לְכָל שְׁאָר אִילָנוֹת. וְנָקַט זֵיתִים לְאַשְׁמוּעִינַן דַּאֲפִלּוּ זֵיתִים שֶׁחַיָּבִים בַּפֵּאָה לֹא מַפְסְקֵי לְבֵית הִלֵּל, וְכָל שֶׁכֵּן שְׁאָר אִילָנוֹת שֶׁאֵין חַיָּבִין בַּפֵּאָה דִּפְשִׁיטָא דְּלֹא מַפְסְקֵי:


שבין הזיתים – and the same law applies for all other kinds of trees, and he (i.e., the anonymous Tanna/teacher of the Mishnah) took [the word] “olives,” to teach us, that even olives are liable in Peah/the corner of the field [to be left for the poor], are not from the legal decisions of the School of Hillel, and all the more so, the rest of the trees which are not liable for Peah/the corner of the field, for it is obvious that they did not make this legal decision.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Peah 3:1:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/3:1:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/3:1:3)


**בֵּית שַׁמַּאי אוֹמְרִים פֵּאָה מִכָּל אֶחָד וְאֶחָד.** מִכָּל מַלְבֵּן וּמַלְבֵּן, הוֹאִיל וְעוֹמְדוֹת הָעֲרוּגוֹת פִּסְקֵי פִּסְקֵי וְאֵין מִתְעָרְבִין זוֹ עִם זוֹ קָסָבְרֵי בֵּית שַׁמַּאי דְּמַפְסְקֵי זֵיתִים. [אֲבָל] הֵיכָא דְרָאשֵׁי שׁוּרוֹת מִתְעָרְבִים מִזֶּה לָזֶה [מוֹדוּ] דְּלֹא מַפְסְקֵי. וּבִזְמַן שֶׁהָאִילָנוֹת מְרֻחָקִים זֶה מִזֶּה עַד שֶׁבְּקַרְקַע בֵּית סְאָה שֶׁהוּא חֲמִשִּׁים אַמָּה עַל חֲמִשִּׁים אַמָּה אֵין בּוֹ עֲשָׂרָה אִילָנוֹת, מוֹדִים בֵּית הִלֵּל שֶׁנּוֹתֵן פֵּאָה לְכָל מַלְבֵּן וּמַלְבֵּן, דְּהוֹאִיל וְהָאִילָנוֹת מְרֻחָקִים נִרְאִין הַדְּבָרִים שֶׁלֹּא בִּשְׁבִיל הָאִילָנוֹת נַעֲשׂוּ הַמַּלְבְּנוֹת. וּבִזְמַן שֶׁהָאִילָנוֹת רְצוּפִים וְיֵשׁ עֶשֶׂר אִילָנוֹת בְּפָחוֹת מִבֵּית סְאָה מוֹדִים בֵּית שַׁמַּאי שֶׁנּוֹתֵן פֵּאָה אַחַת עַל הַכֹּל, לֹא נֶחְלְקוּ אֶלָּא כְּשֶׁיֵּשׁ עֶשֶׂר אִילָנוֹת בְּבֵית סְאָה:


ב"ש אומרים פאה מכל אחד ואחד – from each small garden-bed/plot and garden-bed/plot, since the small garden-beds/plots stand each piece detached and they do combine with one another, as the School of Shammai holds that the olives are detached. But where the beginnings of the rows are combined with each other, they admit that they are not separate. And at the time when the trees are distant from each other until in the ground, there is a field requiring one Se’ah of seed (a square measure), fifty cubits by fifty cubits and there aren’t ten trees in it, the School of Hillel admits that we give Peah for each and every garden-bed, since the trees are far apart, things appear that it is not for the trees that these small garden-beds were made. And when the trees are joined closely and there are ten trees in less than a field requiring one Se’ah of seed, the School of Shammai admits that we give one Peah for everything, they only disputed when there are ten trees in a field requiring one Se’ah of seed.



### Mishnah 2

###### Bartenura on Mishnah Peah 3:2:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/3:2:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/3:2:1)


**הַמְנַמֵּר.** מְלַקֵּט שָׂדֵהוּ מְקוֹמוֹת מְקוֹמוֹת, דְּמֵחֲזֵי כִּמְנוּמָר, לְשׁוֹן וְנָמֵר חֲבַרְבֻּרֹתָיו (ירמיה יג), שֶׁקָּצַר הַתְּבוּאָה שֶׁנִּתְבַּשְּׁלָה רִאשׁוֹן רִאשׁוֹן בַּשָּׂדֶה:


המנמר – he harvests his field place by place, that would speckled, from the [Biblical] language (Jeremiah 13:23): “[Can the Cushite change his skin,] Or the leopard his spots?..,” for he reaped the grain that ripened first, the first of the field.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Peah 3:2:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/3:2:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/3:2:2)


**וְשִׁיֵּר קְלָחִים לַחִים.** אוֹתָן שֶׁלֹּא נִתְבַּשְּׁלוּ:


ושייר קלחים לחים – those that had not ripened.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Peah 3:2:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/3:2:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/3:2:3)


**נוֹתֵן פֵּאָה מִכָּל אֶחָד וְאֶחָד.** כְּשֶׁחוֹזֵר לְלַקֵּט הַקְּלָחִים לַחִים שֶׁשִּׁיֵּר. דְּנִמּוּר מַפְסִיק וְלָאו אַתְחַלְתָּא דִקְצִירָה חֲשִׁיבָא:


נותן פאה לכל אחד ואחד – and when he returns to harvest the moist stalks that he left, those that are speckled interrupts and it is not considered the beginning of the harvest.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Peah 3:2:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/3:2:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/3:2:4)


**בְּזוֹרֵעַ שֶׁבֶת.** יָרָק שֶׁקּוֹרִין לוֹ אניט"ו בְּלַעַ"ז:


בזרוע שבת – vegetation that we call in the foreign language ANITAV.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Peah 3:2:5

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/3:2:5](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/3:2:5)


**שֶׁהוּא נוֹתֵן פֵּאָה לְכָל אַחַת וְאַחַת.** מִפְּנֵי שֶׁאֵין דַּרְכָּן לִזְרֹעַ מֵהֶן שָׂדֶה אַחַת, לְפִיכָךְ חֲשׁוּבִין כָּל אוֹתָן עֲרוּגוֹת כְּאִלּוּ כָּל אַחַת מֵהֶן שָׂדֶה אַחַת. וְשֶׁבֶת וְחַרְדָּל חַיָּבִים בַּפֵּאָה אַף עַל פִּי שֶׁאֵין נוֹתְנִין פֵּאָה לַיָּרָק, מִשּׁוּם דִּלְזֶרַע עֲבִידֵי וּכְמִינֵי זְרָעִים חֲשִׁיבֵי. וַהֲלָכָה כַּחֲכָמִים:


שהוא נותן פאה לכל אחת ואחת – because it is not their manner to sow from them one field; therefore, all of these garden-beds are considered as if each one of them are one field, and dill and mustard are liable for Peah, even though we don’t give a corner of the field for vegetation, because it is used for seeding and it is considered for kinds of seeds, and the Halakha is according to the Sages.



### Mishnah 3

###### Bartenura on Mishnah Peah 3:3:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/3:3:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/3:3:1)


**הַמַּחֲלִיק בְּצָלִים.** אִית דִּמְפָרְשֵׁי לְשׁוֹן וְאָנֹכִי אִישׁ חָלָק (בראשית כו), עַל שֵׁם שֶׁכְּשֶׁנִּתְלְשׁוּ נִשְׁאַר הַמָּקוֹם חָלָק. וְאִית דִּמְפָרְשֵׁי הַמַּחֲלִיק כְּמוֹ הַמְחַלֵּק, שֶׁנּוֹטֵל חֵלֶק מִן הַבְּצָלִים לַחִים לְמָכְרָן בַּשּׁוּק וְחֵלֶק אַחֵר מַנִּיחַ יְבֵשִׁים לַגֹּרֶן לְקִיּוּם:


המחליק בצלים – there are those who interpret the language [in Biblical terms] (Genesis 27:11): “[Jacob answered his mother Rebekah, ‘But my brother Esau is a hairy man] and I am smooth-skinned,’” for when they were detached/plucked, the place remained smooth/empty. And there are those who interpret it as when he levels [a field by taking out crops], when he takes part of the moist/fresh onions to sell them in the market place and another part he leaves the dry ones for the granary for storage.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Peah 3:3:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/3:3:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/3:3:2)


**נוֹתֵן פֵּאָה לְאֵלּוּ לְעַצְמָן.** דְּכִשְׁנֵי מִינֵי חִטִּים דָּמוּ:


נותן פאה לאלו לעצמן – they are like two different kinds of wheat.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Peah 3:3:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/3:3:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/3:3:3)


**וְכֵן בְּכָרֶם.** וְכֵן הַמַּחֲלִיק בְּכֶרֶם דִּינוֹ כְּמַחֲלִיק בִּבְצָלִים:


וכן בכרם – and similarly one who clears the vineyard, its law is like one who clears out the onions.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Peah 3:3:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/3:3:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/3:3:4)


**הַמֵּדֵל נוֹתֵן מִן הַמְשֻׁיָּר עַל מַה שֶׁשִּׁיֵּר.** בִּזְמַן שֶׁהַבְּצָלִים זְרוּעִים רְצוּפִים נוֹטֵל מֵהֶם אֶחָד מִבֵּינְתַּיִם כְּדֵי שֶׁהַנִּשְׁאָרִים יִתְגַדְּלוּ בְּרֶוַח וְיֵעָשׂוּ גַּסִּים. וְזֶהוּ הַנִּקְרָא מֵדֵל, שֶׁדּוֹלֶה וְשׁוֹלֵף אוֹתָם מִמְּקוֹמָם, וּתְנַן אֵיזֶהוּ מֵדֵל, נוֹטֵל אַחַת וּמַנִּיחַ שְׁנַיִם. וְאוֹתָן שֶׁעוֹקֵר לִתֵּן רֶוַח לַנִּשְׁאָרִים אֵינָם חַיָּבִין בַּפֵּאָה, לְפִי שֶׁתַּקָּנַת הַנִּשְׁאָרִים הִיא, הִלְכָּךְ נוֹתֵן פֵּאָה מִן הַנִּשְׁאָר לְבָד וְהַנֶּעֱקַר פָּטוּר מִכְּלוּם דְּלֹא חֲשִׁיבָא קְצִירָה:


המדל נותן מן המשוייר על מה ששייר – at the time that the onions are sown close together, he takes from one from among them in order that the remainder will grow with open space and become large. That is what is called to thin out the vineyard by lifting up and loosening them from their place and our Mishnah (Tractate Shevi’it, Chapter 4, Mishnah 4) teaches: “Who is one who levels his field? He takes one and leaves two,” and those which he uproots to give space to the rest are not liable for Peah because it is for the repair of the remainder, therefore, he gives Peah from the remainder alone and what has been uprooted is exempt from anything and it is not considered reaping.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Peah 3:3:5

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/3:3:5](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/3:3:5)


**הַמַּחֲלִיק מֵאַחַת יַד.** כְּלוֹמַר מֵעִנְיָן אֶחָד, אוֹ כֻּלּוֹ לַגֹּרֶן אוֹ כֻּלּוֹ לַשּׁוּק. וְרַמְבַּ"ם פֵּרֵשׁ מֵאַחַת יַד מִמָּקוֹם אֶחָד, שֶׁהַחֵלֶק שֶׁנּוֹטֵל לִמְכֹּר בַּשּׁוּק אֵינוֹ נוֹטֵל אוֹתוֹ מִכָּאן וּמִכָּאן אֶלָּא כֻּלּוֹ מִצַּד אֶחָד:


המחליק מאחת יד – that is to say, from one matter, or all of it for the granary or all of it to the market. And Maimonides explains “with a unity of hand” (i.e., all of them for one purpose), that the part which he takes to sell in the marketplace he does take from here and there, but all of it is from one side.



### Mishnah 4

###### Bartenura on Mishnah Peah 3:4:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/3:4:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/3:4:1)


**הָאִמָּהוֹת שֶׁל בְּצָלִים.** בְּצָלִים גְּדוֹלִים שֶׁמַּנִּיחִין אוֹתָן לְגַדֵּל זֶרַע, וּמִתּוֹךְ שֶׁמִּשְׁתָּהִין בָּאָרֶץ לֹא חָזוּ לַאֲכִילָה אֶלָּא עַל יְדֵי הַדְּחַק, וּלְכָךְ פָּטַר רַבִּי יוֹסֵי. וְאֵין הֲלָכָה כְּמוֹתוֹ:


האמהות של בצלים – large onions that we leave them to grow seeds, and as a result that they remain in the ground and are not worthy for eating other than in the case of an emergency, therefore, Rabbi Yosi exempted them, but the Halakha is not according to him.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Peah 3:4:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/3:4:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/3:4:2)


**מַלְבְּנוֹת הַבְּצָלִים שֶׁבֵּין הַיָּרָק.** סָבַר רַבִּי יוֹסֵי דְּיָרָק כְּזֶרַע אַחֵר דָּמִי, שֶׁאֵין דֶּרֶךְ בְּנֵי אָדָם לִהְיוֹת מַכְנִיס בְּצָלִים בֵּין הַיָּרָק, וּתְנַן לְעֵיל בְּפֶרֶק שֵׁנִי דְּזֶרַע אַחֵר מַפְסִיק. וְאֵין הֲלָכָה כְּרַבִּי יוֹסֵי:


מלבנות הבצלים שבין הירק – Rabbi Yosi holds that the vegetable is considered like a different seed, for it is not the manner for people to bring in onions among the vegetables, nd it is taught in the Mishna above in Chapter two (Mishna one) that a different seed interrupts, but the Halakha is not according to Rabbi Yosi.



### Mishnah 5

###### Bartenura on Mishnah Peah 3:5:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/3:5:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/3:5:1)


**שֶׁלָּקְחוּ אֶת הָאִילָן.** מֵהֲנָךְ אִילָנוֹת דְּחָשִׁיב בְּפֶרֶק קַמָּא שֶׁחַיָּבִים בְּפֵאָה:


שלקחו את האילן – from those trees that are considered in the first chapter [of our Tractate, Mishnah 5] that are liable for Peah.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Peah 3:5:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/3:5:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/3:5:2)


**הַמּוֹכֵר קִלְחֵי אִילָן.** שָׁרְשֵׁי צְמָחִים שֶׁחַיָּבִין בַּפֵּאָה, וְלֹא מָכַר לוֹ הַקַּרְקַע, נוֹתֵן הַלּוֹקֵחַ פֵּאָה מִכָּל אֶחָד וְאֶחָד:


המוכר קלחי אילן – the roots of the plants that are liable for Peah, but he did not sell him the actual ground, the purchaser gives Peah/corner of the field [for the poor] for each one of them.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Peah 3:5:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/3:5:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/3:5:3)


**בִּזְמַן שֶׁלֹּא שִׁיֵּר.** כְּלוֹמַר אִם לֹא הִתְחִיל בַּעַל הַשָּׂדֶה לִקְצֹר אוֹ לְלַקֵּט פֵּרוֹת, אָז הוּא דְחַיָּב הַלּוֹקֵחַ לִתֵּן פֵּאָה. אֲבָל אִם הִתְחִיל בַּעַל הַשָּׂדֶה לִקְצֹר שָׂדֵהוּ קֹדֶם שֶׁמָּכַר אֶת אֵלּוּ, שֶׁנִּשְׁאַר מִן הַשָּׂדֶה שֶׁלֹּא נִקְצַר אוֹ שֶׁלֹּא נִלְקַט, בַּעַל הַשָּׂדֶה הוּא הַנּוֹתֵן פֵּאָה עַל הַכֹּל, דִּמְכִּי אַתְחִיל בַּקְּצִירָה אוֹ בִּלְקִיטַת הַפֵּרוֹת אִיחַיָּב בַּפֵּאָה שֶׁל כָּל הַשָּׂדֶה. וְרַבִּי יְהוּדָה לְפֵרוּשֵׁי מִלְּתֵיהּ דְּתָנָא קַמָּא אָתָא, וְכֵן הֲלָכָה:


בזמן שלא שייר – that is to say, if the owner of the field had not begun to reap or harvest the fruit, then the purchaser is liable to give Peah, but if the owner of the field had begun to reap his field before he sold these, what remains from the field that was not reaped or harvested , the owner of the field is the one who gives the Peah on everything, for since he had begun in reaping or the harvesting of the fruit, he becomes liable for Peah on all of the field. But Rabbi Yehuda comes to explain the words of the First Tanna/teacher, and such is the Halakha.



### Mishnah 6

###### Bartenura on Mishnah Peah 3:6:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/3:6:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/3:6:1)


**קַרְקַע בֵּית רֹבַע.** מָקוֹם שֶׁרָאוּי לִזְרֹעַ בּוֹ רֹבַע הַקַּב. וּפֵרְשׁוּ בוֹ שֶׁהוּא עֶשֶׂר אַמּוֹת וּמֶחֱצָה עַל עֶשֶׂר אַמּוֹת בְּקֵרוּב:


קרקע בית רובע – a place where it is appropriate to sow in it one-quarter of a Kab, and they explained it as ten and one-half cubits by ten cubits by proportion.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Peah 3:6:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/3:6:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/3:6:2)


**הָעוֹשָׂה סָאתַיִם.** רַבִּי יְהוֹשֻׁעַ לֹא אָזֵיל בָּתַר זְרִיעָה, אֶלָּא קַרְקַע הַמּוֹצִיאָה סָאתַיִם שֶׁהֵן שְׁנֵים עָשָׂר קַבִּין:


העושה סאתים – Rabbi Yehoshua does not follow after sowing, but rather the ground that produces two Se’ah which is twelve Kabs.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Peah 3:6:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/3:6:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/3:6:3)


**לִקְצֹר וְלִשְׁנוֹת.** דֶּרֶךְ הַקּוֹצְרִים אוֹחֵז מְלֹא כַפּוֹ מִן הַקָּמָה וְקוֹצֵר, כְּדִכְתִיב (תהלים קכט) שֶׁלֹּא מִלֵּא כַפּוֹ קוֹצֵר. וְאִם יֵשׁ בַּקָּמָה כְּדֵי לְמַלֹּאת כַּפּוֹ שְׁנֵי פְעָמִים חַיֶּבֶת בְּפֵאָה:


לקצור ולשנות – it is the manner of reapers that he grasps a palm’s worth from the flour and reaps, as it is written (Psalms 129:7): “that affords no handful for the reaper, [no armful for the gatherer of sheaves],” that the reaper did not have a handful. And if there is among the standing corn in order to fill his hand twice, he is liable for Peah.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Peah 3:6:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/3:6:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/3:6:4)


**קַרְקַע כָּל שֶׁהוּא חַיֶּבֶת בַּפֵּאָה.** דִּסְבִירָא לֵיהּ פְּאַת שָׂדְךָ כָּל שֶׁהוּא מַשְׁמַע, וּפָלֵיג אַכֻּלְּהוּ:


קרקע כל שהוא חייבת בפאה – as he holds, “the corner of your field” (Leviticus 19:9) is implied, and he is in dispute with everyone.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Peah 3:6:5

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/3:6:5](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/3:6:5)


**וּבַבִּכּוּרִים.** דִּכְתִיב בְּהוּ אַדְמָתְךָ. וְהַנֵּי מִלֵּי בְּבִכּוּרֵי חִטָּה וּשְׂעוֹרָה, אֲבָל בְּבִכּוּרִים שֶׁל אִילָן אֵינוֹ חַיָּב אֶלָּא אִם כֵּן יֵשׁ לוֹ קַרְקַע שֵׁשׁ עֶשְׂרֵה אַמּוֹת סָבִיב לָאִילָן, שֶׁהוּא שִׁעוּר יְנִיקָתוֹ:


ובכורים – as it is written regarding them “your land” (Exodus 23:19, 24:36). And these words concern the first-fruits of wheat and barley, but the first-fruits of trees – he is not liable other than if he has land sixteen cubits around the tree which is the measure of its absorption.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Peah 3:6:6

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/3:6:6](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/3:6:6)


**וְלִכְתֹּב עָלֶיהָ פְּרוֹזְבּוּל.** הִלֵּל תִּקֵּן פְּרוֹזְבּוּל, כְּשֶׁרָאָה שֶׁהָיוּ נִמְנָעִים מִלְּהַלְווֹת לָעֲנִיִּים מִפְּנֵי הַשְּׁבִיעִית שֶׁמְּשַׁמֶּטֶת הַחוֹב, תִּקֵּן שֶׁיְּהֵא מוֹסֵר שִׁטְרוֹתָיו לְבֵית דִּין, וְכוֹתֵב, מוֹסֵר אֲנִי לָכֶם פְּלוֹנִי וּפְלוֹנִי הַדַּיָּנִין כָּל חוֹב שֶׁיֵּשׁ לִי שֶׁאֶגְבֶנּוּ כָּל זְמַן שֶׁאֶרְצֶה, וּמִשֶּׁכָּתְבוּ לוֹ שְׁטָר זֶה שׁוּב אֵין שְׁבִיעִית מְשַׁמֶּטֶת חוֹבוֹ. וְדַוְקָא כְּשֶׁיֵּשׁ קַרְקַע כָּל דְּהוּא לַלּוֶֹה דְּאָז חָשִׁיב כְּאִלּוּ הַחוֹב גָּבוּי כְּבָר בְּיַד בֵּית דִּין וְתוּ לֹא קָרֵינָא בֵיהּ לֹא יִגֹּשׂ, וְחָשְׁבִינַן לְקַרְקַע כָּל שֶׁהוּא כְּאִלּוּ שָׁוֶה כָּל הַחוֹב, דְּאֵין אוֹנָאָה לְקַרְקָעוֹת:


ולכתוב עליה פרוזבול – Hillel instituted the Prosbul, when he saw that people were prevented from making loans to the poor because the [upcoming] seventh year cancels the debt, he instituted that one should transfer their documents to the Jewish court and write: “I transfer to you, so-and-so and so-and-so the judges every liable that I have that I will collect at the time when I desire,” and when they have written for him this document the seventh year does not cancel his debt (see Mishnah Shevi’it, Chapter 10, Mishnayot 3-4), and specifically when the borrower has a parcel of land, even a bit, and it is then considered as if the debt is collected already in the hand of the Jewish court, and further, we don’t call it (Deuteronomy 15:2): “he shall not dun,” and we consider the land, however small, as if it is worth the entire debt as there is no overreaching for land.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Peah 3:6:7

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/3:6:7](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/3:6:7)


**וְלִקְנוֹת עִמָּהּ נְכָסִים שֶׁאֵין לָהֶם אַחֲרָיוּת.** דְּמִטַּלְטְלִין נִקְנִין אַגַּב קַרְקַע, בְּכֶסֶף בִּשְׁטָר וּבַחֲזָקָה, כְּדִתְנַן בְּפֶרֶק קַמָּא דְקִדּוּשִׁין, וְשָׁם מְפֹרָשׁ:


ולקנות עמה נכסים שאין להם אחריות – for movables are acquired with the land, by money, by documentation, or by claim of undisturbed possession (during a legally fixed period).



### Mishnah 7

###### Bartenura on Mishnah Peah 3:7:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/3:7:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/3:7:1)


**שְׁכִיב מְרַע.** הַשּׁוֹכֵב עַל מִטָּתוֹ מֵחֲמַת חֹלִי:


שכיב מרע – he who lies on his bed on account of illness.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Peah 3:7:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/3:7:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/3:7:2)


**שִׁיֵּר קַרְקַע כָּל שֶׁהוּא.** כָּל הַנֵּי שִׁיֵּר קַרְקַע כָּל שֶׁהוּא. דִּתְנַן בְּמַתְנִיתִין מִכָּאן וְעַד סוֹף פִּרְקִין, לָאו דַּוְקָא קַרְקַע, דְּהוּא הַדִּין מִטַּלְטְלִים כָּל שֶׁהֵן. אֶלָּא אַיְדֵי דְּתָנָא רֵישָׁא גַּבֵּי פֵּאָה וּבִכּוּרִים וּפְרוֹזְבּוּל קַרְקַע כָּל שֶׁהוּא, תָּנָא בְּכָל הֲנָךְ נַמִּי קַרְקַע כָּל שֶׁהוּא:


שייר קרקע כל שהוא – all these he left a bit of land, as is taught in our Mishnah, from here until the end of our chapter, not exactly land as the same law applies with a bit of movables, but since the first part of the Mishnah teaches regarding the corner and First Fruits and Prosbul/a declaration made in court before the execution of a loan to the effect that the law of limitation by the entrance of the Sabbatical year shall not apply to the loan to be transacted – a bit of land is taught in all of these are also a bit of land.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Peah 3:7:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/3:7:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/3:7:3)


**מַתְּנָתוֹ קַיֶּמֶת.** אִם קָנוּ מִיָּדוֹ עַל הַמַּתָּנָה, אַף עַל פִּי שֶׁעָמַד מֵחָלְיוֹ אֵינוֹ יָכוֹל לַחֲזֹר בּוֹ, הוֹאִיל וְשִׁיֵּר לְעַצְמוֹ כָּל שֶׁהוּא גַּלֵּי דַעְתֵּיהּ דְּלָאו מֵחֲמַת מִיתָה כָּתַב הַמַּתָּנָה:


מתנתו קיימת – if they purchased from his hand on the gift even though he rose from his illness, he cannot retract, since he left for himself a bit, he revealed his thinking that it was not on account of death that he wrote the gift.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Peah 3:7:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/3:7:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/3:7:4)


**לֹא שִׁיֵּר קַרְקַע כָּל שֶׁהוּא.** לְעַצְמוֹ, מִסְּתָמָא לֹא שָׁבִיק אִינָשׁ נַפְשֵׁיהּ עַרְטִילָאִי, וְאִם לֹא שֶׁהָיָה חוֹשֵׁב וַדַּאי שֶׁיָּמוּת לֹא הָיָה נוֹתֵן כָּל נְכָסָיו, הִלְכָּךְ אִם עָמַד חוֹזֵר, וַאֲפִלּוּ קָנוּ מִיָּדוֹ:


לא שייר קרקע כל שהוא – for himself, from something undefined, a person does not leave himself poorly dressed, and if he had not thought that he would certainly die, he would not give all his possessions, therefore if he stood, he retracts and even if they purchased it from his hand.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Peah 3:7:5

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/3:7:5](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/3:7:5)


**וְכָתַב לְאִשְׁתּוֹ קַרְקַע כָּל שֶׁהוּא אִבְּדָה כְּתֻבָּתָהּ.** בִּשְׁכִיב מְרַע מַיְרֵי, שֶׁמְּחַלֵּק נְכָסָיו לְבָנָיו, וְכָתַב לְאִשְׁתּוֹ חֵלֶק בֵּין הַבָּנִים, וְשָׁמְעָה אִיהִי וְשָׁתְקָה וְלֹא אָמְרָה כְּתֻבָּתִי אֲנִי גוֹבָה אוֹ כַּיּוֹצֵא בָזֶה, אִבְּדָה כְּתֻבָּתָהּ:


וכתב לאשתו קרקע כל שהוא אבדה כתובתה – we are speaking of someone near death who distributes his possessions to his children and writes to his wife a portion among the children, and she herself heard and was silent and didn’t say, “I am collecting my Ketubah” or something similar, she loses her Ketubah.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Peah 3:7:6

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/3:7:6](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/3:7:6)


**רַבִּי יוֹסֵי אוֹמֵר אִם קִבְּלָה עָלֶיהָ.** שֶׁתְּהֵא שֻׁתֶּפֶת בֵּין הַבָּנִים, אֲפִלּוּ לֹא קָנוּ מִיָּדָהּ, וְהוּא לֹא צִוָּה שֶׁיִּכְתְּבוּ כָּךְ בְּפָנֶיהָ, אִבְּדָה כְּתֻבָּתָהּ וְשׁוּב אֵינָהּ יְכוֹלָה לַחֲזֹר בָּהּ. וְאֵין הֲלָכָה כְּרַבִּי יוֹסֵי:


רבי יוסי אומר אם קבלה עליה – that she would be a partner among the children, and even if they did not purchase from her hand, and he did not command that they should write such in her presence, she lost her Ketubah and furthermore she is not able to retract, but the Halakha is not according to Rabbi Yosi.



### Mishnah 8

###### Bartenura on Mishnah Peah 3:8:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/3:8:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/3:8:1)


**הַכּוֹתֵב כָּל נְכָסָיו לְעַבְדּוֹ.** שֶׁכָּתַב כָּל נְכָסַי נְתוּנִים לִפְלוֹנִי עַבְדִּי, הֲרֵי הָעֶבֶד בִּכְלַל הַנְּכָסִים וְנָתַן לוֹ אֶת עַצְמוֹ. וְהָא דְקָאָמַר עַבְדִּי הַיְנוּ עַבְדִּי שֶׁהָיָה כְּבָר:


הכותב כל נכסיו לעבדו – that he wrote, “all my property to so-and-so my slave,” for the slave is include in the property and he (the owner) gave him his selfhood and that which when it states, “my slave” that is my slave that was already.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Peah 3:8:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/3:8:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/3:8:2)


**שִׁיֵּר קַרְקַע כָּל שֶׁהוּא לֹא יָצָא בֶן חוֹרִין.** דְּכָל הֵיכָא דְנָחִית לְשִׁיּוּרָא אָמְרִינַן לְדִידֵיהּ נַמִּי שִׁיֵּר, וְכִי אָמַר כָּל נְכָסַי לְךָ אַשְּׁאָר נְכָסִים קָאָמַר וְלַחֲנוּפֵי לֵיהּ קָאָתֵי, וְלֹא שִׁחְרְרֵיהּ, כֵּיוָן דְּלֹא אָמַר לֵיהּ עַצְמְךָ וּנְכָסַי. וַאֲפִלּוּ אֵין לוֹ שׁוּם נְכָסִים אֶלָּא הָעֶבֶד וְהַקַּרְקַע שֶׁשִּׁיֵּר, מִכָּל מָקוֹם לֹא יָצָא לְחֵרוּת, דְּכֵיוָן דְּאֵין מִתְקַיֵּם דִּבּוּר כָּל נְכָסַי דְּהָא אָתָא חוּץ וּבָטֵל לֵיהּ, דְּלֹא פַּלְגִינַן דִּבּוּרָא, וַהֲוֵי בָּטֵל לְגַמְרֵי וְלֹא קָנָה לֹא עַצְמוֹ וְלֹא נְכָסִים:


שייר קרקע כל שהוא לא יצא בן חורין – all where he puts down a measurement, we say that he also left to himself, and when he said: “all my property is yours,” he is speaking of the rest of his property, but he deceived him that he came and not to free him. Since he (i.e., the owner) did not say to him “yourself and my property, and even if he doesn’t have any property other than the slave and the land that he left, nevertheless, he (i.e., the slave) does not go out to freedom, for since the word “all my property” is not fulfilled, for it comes as “except for” and he annuls it and we don’t argue his words and they are completely annulled, and he (i.e., the slave) did not acquire his self-food nor the property.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Peah 3:8:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/3:8:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/3:8:3)


**רַבִּי שִׁמְעוֹן אוֹמֵר לְעוֹלָם הוּא בֶן חוֹרִין.** בֵּין אֵין לוֹ נְכָסִים אֲחֵרִים אֶלָּא הָעֶבֶד וְהַקַּרְקַע שֶׁשִּׁיֵּר, וּבֵין יֵשׁ לוֹ נְכָסִים אֲחֵרִים, לְעוֹלָם הוּא בֶן חוֹרִין. וּמוֹקְמִינַן דִּבּוּר כָּל נְכָסַי בָּעֶבֶד לְבַדּוֹ כְּשֶׁאֵין לוֹ נְכָסִים אֲחֵרִים, וְלֹא אָמְרִינַן דִּבְמַה שֶּׁאָמַר חוּץ בִּטֵּל דִּבּוּרוֹ שֶּׁאָמַר כָּל נְכָסַי, דְּפַלְגִּינַן דִּבּוּרָא, וּמְקַיְמִינַן לֵיהּ בָּעֶבֶד, וְיָצָא בֶן חוֹרִין:


ר"ש אומר לעולם הו בן חורין – whether he has no property other than the slave and the land that he left, or whether he has other property, he (i.e., the slave) is always a free man, and we establish the word, “all my property” with the slave alone, when he doesn’t have other property, and we don’t say when he said, “except for,” this nullifies his word that he said, “all my property,” for we separate his word, and we fulfill him regarding his slave and he goes out as a free man.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Peah 3:8:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/3:8:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/3:8:4)


**חוּץ מֵאֶחָד מֵרִבּוֹא.** וְלֹא פֵרֵשׁ מַהוּ מְשַׁיֵּר, דְּהָתָם אָמְרִינַן דְּאֶחָד מֵרִבּוֹא דְּקָאָמַר הַיְנוּ עֶבֶד, וְאִיהוּ לֹא חָשִׁיב לֵיהּ אֶלָּא כְּאֶחָד מֵרִבּוֹא בִּנְכָסִים, וְאַף עַל גַּב דְּשָׁוֵי טְפֵי. וְאֵין הֲלָכָה כְּרַבִּי שִׁמְעוֹן:


חוץ מאחד מרבוא – and he didn’t explain what he leaves aside, but there we said that one-ten-thousandth part that he said, is the servant, and he is not considered other than one of one-ten-thousandth of his property and even though he is worth more, but the Halakha is not according to Rabbi Shimon.



## Chapter 4


### Mishnah 1

###### Bartenura on Mishnah Peah 4:1:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/4:1:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/4:1:1)


**הַפֵּאָה נִתֶּנֶת בִּמְחֻבָּר.** דִּכְתִיב (ויקרא יט) לֶעָנִי וְלַגֵּר תַּעֲזֹב, הַנַּח לִפְנֵיהֶם וְהֵם יָבוֹזוּ:


הפאה ניתנת במחובר – as it is written (Leviticus 19:10): “[You shall not pick your vineyard bare, or gather the fallen fruit of your vineyard;] you shall leave them for the poor and the stranger: …],” lay it down before them and they will plunder it.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Peah 4:1:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/4:1:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/4:1:2)


**בְּדָלִית.** גֶּפֶן הַמֻּדְלֵית עַל גַּבֵּי עֵצִים אוֹ עַל גַּבֵּי אִילָנוֹת:


בדלית – a vine that is suspended on the wood or on the trees.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Peah 4:1:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/4:1:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/4:1:3)


**מוֹרִיד וּמְחַלֵּק.** דִּכְתִיב (שם) תַּעֲזֹב אוֹתָם, אוֹתָם שֶׁאֵין בָּהֶם סַכָּנָה אַתָּה עוֹזֵב לִפְנֵיהֶם וְאִי אַתָּה עוֹזֵב אוֹתָם שֶׁיֵּשׁ סַכָּנָה בַּעֲלִיָּתָם, אֶלָּא מוֹרִיד מִן הָאִילָן וּמְחַלֵּק לָהֶם:


מוריד ומחלק – as it is written (Leviticus 19:10): “you shall leave them [for the poor and the stranger…],”those which have no danger you leave before them, and if you do not leave them when there is a danger ascending [to get] them, but one brings them down from the tree and distributes it to them.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Peah 4:1:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/4:1:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/4:1:4)


**בַּחֲלִיקֵי אֱגוֹזִים.** עַל שֵׁם שֶׁאִילָנוֹת שֶׁל אֱגוֹזִים חֲלָקִים וְאֵין בָּהֶם קְשָׁרִים כִּשְׁאָר אִילָנוֹת קְרוּיִן חֲלִיקֵי אֱגוֹזִים. וְאֵין הֲלָכָה כְּרַבִּי שִׁמְעוֹן:


כחליקי אגוזים – on the name of the smooth nut-trees and they have no connection like the rest of the trees that are called nut trees too smooth for climbing, but the Halakha is not according to Rabbi Shimon.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Peah 4:1:5

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/4:1:5](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/4:1:5)


**אֲפִלּוּ תִשְׁעִים וְתִשְׁעָה.** אַרֵישָׁא קָאֵי, בְּפֵאָה הַנִּתֶּנֶת בִּמְחֻבָּר:


אפילו תשעים ותשעה – it refers to the beginning of the Mishnah, that Peah/the corner is given unharvested.



### Mishnah 2

###### Bartenura on Mishnah Peah 4:2:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/4:2:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/4:2:1)


**בְּדָלִית וּבְדֶקֶל.** שֶׁמִּצְוָתָן בְּתָלוּשׁ:


בדלית ובדקל – that their observance is with something detached.



### Mishnah 3

###### Bartenura on Mishnah Peah 4:3:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/4:3:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/4:3:1)


**נָטַל.** אֶחָד מִן הָעֲנִיִּים מִקְצַת פֵּאָה שֶׁלִּקֵּט כְּבָר וְזָרַק לוֹ עַל הַשְּׁאָר כְּדֵי לִקְנֹת:


נטל – one of the poor people [took] part of the Peah that he had already gleaned and he threw/tossed it on the rest in order to acquire it.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Peah 4:3:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/4:3:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/4:3:2)


**אֵין לוֹ בָהּ כְּלוּם.** אֲפִלּוּ בְּמַה שֶּׁלִּקֵּט, דְּקוֹנְסִין אוֹתוֹ וּמוֹצִיאִין מִמֶּנּוּ הַתָּלוּשׁ וְהַמְחֻבָּר:


אין לו בה כלום – even with what he had gleaned, for we fine him and remove from him [both] the detached and attached.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Peah 4:3:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/4:3:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/4:3:3)


**נָפַל לוֹ עָלֶיהָ וְכוּ'.** אַף עַל גַּב דְּאַרְבַּע אַמּוֹת שֶׁל אָדָם קוֹנוֹת לוֹ בְּסִמְטָא וּבְצִדֵּי רְשׁוּת הָרַבִּים, הָכָא בִּשְׂדֵה חֲבֵרוֹ לֹא תַקִּינוּ לֵיהּ רַבָּנָן שֶׁיִּקְנוּ לוֹ אַרְבַּע אַמּוֹתָיו. אִי נַמִּי כֵּיוָן דְּנָפַל עָלֶיהָ גָּלֵּי דַעְתֵּיהּ דְּבִנְפִילָה נִיחָא לֵיהּ דִּלְקְנֵי, בְּאַרְבַּע אַמּוֹת לֹא נִיחָא לֵיהּ דִּלְקְנֵי:


נפל לו עליה וכו' – even though four cubits of a person acquires for him in an alley/recess and at the sides of the public domain; here in the field of his fellow the Rabbis did not establish for him that his four cubits would acquire for him; alternatively, since he fell upon it he revealed his intention that through falling it is appropriate that it would acquire for him; in the four cubits it is not appropriate for him that he would acquire it.



### Mishnah 5

###### Bartenura on Mishnah Peah 4:5:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/4:5:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/4:5:1)


**שָׁלֹשׁ אַבְעָיוֹת בַּיּוֹם.** שָׁלֹשׁ פְּעָמִים מִתְגַּלֶּה בַּעַל הַבַּיִת וְנִרְאֶה בְּתוֹךְ שָׂדֵהוּ כְּדֵי שֶׁיִּקְחוּ הָעֲנִיִּים פֵּאָה. אַבְעָיוֹת לְשׁוֹן גִּלּוּי, נִבְעוּ מַצְפֻּנָיו (עובדיה א) מְתַרְגְּמִינַן אִגַּלְיָן מַטְמְרוֹהִי:


שלש אבעיות ביום – three times the owner revealed/exposed and appeared in his field in order that the poor could take Peah. The word אבעיות – means revealing (Obadiah 1:6): “[How thoroughly rifled is Esau,] How ransacked his hoards!” We translate this [in Aramaic] as revealing a hidden object.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Peah 4:5:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/4:5:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/4:5:2)


**בַּשַּׁחַר.** מִפְּנֵי הַמֵּנִיקוֹת שֶׁבְּנֵיהֶם יְשֵׁנִים בַּשַּׁחַר וְאָז יֵשׁ לָהֶם פְּנַאי לִלְקֹט:


בשחר – because of those nursing among them who are sleeping in the morning, and then they have the free time to glean.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Peah 4:5:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/4:5:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/4:5:3)


**וּבַחֲצוֹת.** מִפְּנֵי הַתִּינוֹקוֹת שֶׁדַּרְכָּן לָצֵאת בַּחֲצוֹת וְהוֹלְכִים לִלְקֹט פֵּאָה:


ובחצות – because of the young children who are accustomed to go out at noon and walk to glean Peah.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Peah 4:5:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/4:5:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/4:5:4)


**וּבַמִּנְחָה.** מִפְּנֵי הַזְּקֵנִים הַהוֹלְכִים עַל מִשְׁעַנְתָּם וְאֵין מַגִּיעִים לַשָּׂדֶה עַד שְׁעַת הַמִּנְחָה:


ובמנחה – because of the elderly who walk with their crutches and they don’t arrive to the field until the afternoon.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Peah 4:5:5

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/4:5:5](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/4:5:5)


**שֶׁלֹּא יִפְחֲתוּ.** מִשָּׁלֹשׁ עִתִּים הַלָּלוּ. וַהֲלָכָה שֶׁאֵין נוֹתְנִין פֵּאָה אֶלָּא בְּשָׁלֹשׁ עִתִּים הַלָּלוּ, אֵין פּוֹחֲתִין מֵהֶן וְאֵין מוֹסִיפִין עֲלֵיהֶם:


שלא יפחתו – from these three times. And the Halakha is that we don’t distribute Peah other than at these three times; we don’t make it any less or any more than this.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Peah 4:5:6

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/4:5:6](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Peah/r/4:5:6)


**שֶׁל בֵּית נָמֵר.** שֵׁם מָקוֹם, כְּדִכְתִיב (במדבר לב) אֶת בֵּית נִמְרָה. קוֹשְׁרִים חֶבֶל בְּצַד הַקָּמָה וְקוֹצְרִים וְהוֹלְכִים עַד שֶׁיִּכְלֶה הַחֶבֶל, וּמַנִּיחִים פֵּאָה לְכָל הַחֶבֶל, וְחוֹזְרִים וְקוֹשְׁרִים וּמַנִּיחִים פֵּאָה, וְהַיְנוּ מִכָּל אֻמָּן וְאֻמָּן, כְּלוֹמַר מִכָּל שׁוּרָה וְשׁוּרָה, וְעַל זֶה מַזְכִּירִין אוֹתָן לְשֶׁבַח:


של בית נמר – It is the name of a place, as it is written (Numbers 32:36): “Beth-nimrah, [and Beth-haran as fortified towns or as enclosures for flocks].” They tie a rope at the same of the standing corn and continue reaping until the rope runs out and place the Peah the entire rope, and they go back and tie and place the Peah, that is from each and every artisan, that is to say, from each and every row and for this reason they mention these for praise.



### Mishnah 6
